# Proposed OEIS comments: A189644 to A199945

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A189644

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A189644>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4751](../papers/04501-05000/04751-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 9*a(n-1) - 6*a(n-2) - 8*a(n-3) + 16*a(n-4).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(3 + 6*x - 16*x^2 + 16*x^3) / (1 - 9*x + 6*x^2 + 8*x^3 - 16*x^4). - _Colin Barker_, May 02 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The count is a permanent: the cells are permuted and each one moves by one of the offsets the name lists. Number the cells in row-major order and let each choose its image; an offset moves a cell by a bounded number of places in that order, so all that has to be carried is which images inside a window of 9 consecutive positions are already taken. That makes a(n) a walk count on 25 states, hence C-finite of order at most that, and checking the recurrence above is a finite exact computation that holds for n > 3. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```

```

## A190171

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A190171>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [12464](../papers/12001-12500/12464-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture D-finite with recurrence (n+2)*a(n) +(-n+1)*a(n-1) +2*(-2*n-1)*a(n-2) +9*a(n-3) +(-n+1)*a(n-4) -9*a(n-5) +2*(-2*n+5)*a(n-6) +(-n+1)*a(n-7) +(n-4)*a(n-8)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jul 22 2022

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 5, so the recurrence holds for n > 5. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A193641

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A193641>  
Result obtained: **13 September 2026**  
Paper: [5076](../papers/05001-05500/05076-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Or yet empirical: row sums of triangle
> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) + a(n-3).
> Empirical: G.f.: -x*(1+x+x^2) / ( -1+2*x+x^3 ); a(n) = A008998(n-3) + A008998(n-2) + A008998(n-1). - _R. J. Mathar_, Feb 19 2015

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 5 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 0. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A193704>  
Result obtained: **13 September 2026**  
Paper: [2424](../papers/02001-02500/02424-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 3*a(n-1) +4*a(n-2) +a(n-3) -10*a(n-4) +10*a(n-5) +97*a(n-6) +202*a(n-7) +211*a(n-8) +184*a(n-9) +359*a(n-10) +772*a(n-11) +1020*a(n-12) +803*a(n-13) +4*a(n-14) -618*a(n-15) +491*a(n-16) +3674*a(n-17) +6991*a(n-18) +5747*a(n-19) -3079*a(n-20) -14036*a(n-21) -19019*a(n-22) -14576*a(n-23) -5701*a(n-24) +980*a(n-25) -3128*a(n-26) -12159*a(n-27) -19677*a(n-28) -21113*a(n-29) -11499*a(n-30) -3507*a(n-31) +1809*a(n-32) +1619*a(n-33) -5989*a(n-34) -11700*a(n-35) -12527*a(n-36) -9260*a(n-37) -4560*a(n-38) +1240*a(n-39) +7*a(n-40) +712*a(n-41) -65*a(n-42) -1846*a(n-43) -115*a(n-44) -1095*a(n-45) -734*a(n-46) -22*a(n-47) -851*a(n-48) +140*a(n-49) +121*a(n-50) +246*a(n-51) +370*a(n-52) +66*a(n-53) +7*a(n-54) -99*a(n-55) -84*a(n-56) -12*a(n-57).

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 289 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 0. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A194932

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A194932>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [5216](../papers/05001-05500/05216-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 171*n - 127 for n>2.
> Empirical g.f.: x*(64 + 89*x + 16*x^2 + 2*x^3) / (1 - x)^2. - _Colin Barker_, May 05 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This is true, and it is exact rather than eventual guesswork. The shape is fixed and it is the alphabet 0..n that grows, so this is not a walk count. The condition only ever compares two entries, so adding a constant to every entry keeps an array admissible: the arrays fall into translation classes, and a class whose largest and smallest entries differ by s contributes max(0, n+1-s) arrays with entries in 0..n. There are finitely many classes, because any two cells are joined by a path of at most D edges and each edge moves the value by at most d, so no class has s > d*D. For n at least that bound every term is positive and a(n) is exactly linear, so computing a(n) at two points fixes it for good. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [10496](../papers/10001-10500/10496-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) +a(n-3) -2*a(n-4) -a(n-5) +a(n-6)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 625 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 7. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

## A197230

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A197230>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [1298](../papers/01001-01500/01298-DISPROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) +3*a(n-2) +38*a(n-3) +40*a(n-4) +10*a(n-5) -150*a(n-6) -375*a(n-7) -175*a(n-8) +637*a(n-9) +2013*a(n-10) +1368*a(n-11) -1614*a(n-12) -4361*a(n-13) -4043*a(n-14) +946*a(n-15) +4156*a(n-16) +3551*a(n-17) +552*a(n-18) -881*a(n-19) -629*a(n-20) -1033*a(n-21) +508*a(n-22).

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This empirical recurrence is false. The entry publishes 22 terms with offset 1, so the recurrence first says something at n = 23, and it already fails there: the true value is 1327965802062332 and the recurrence gives 1327965802062198. The sequence is a transfer-matrix count, hence C-finite, and its true minimal recurrence has order 25; its first 22 coefficients are exactly the ones above, so the line is the correct recurrence with its last three terms dropped. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

> Empirical: a(n) = 8*a(n-1) + 4*a(n-2) - 2*a(n-3) - 8*a(n-4) for n>6.
> Empirical g.f.: x*(2 - 2*x - 7*x^2 - 3*x^3 + 5*x^4 + 4*x^5) / (1 - 8*x - 4*x^2 + 2*x^3 + 8*x^4). - _Colin Barker_, Mar 02 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 23 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 6. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
